# Followup index

Consolidated index of post-MVP work filed across Phase Y + Phase Z by both
Session A (daemon + CLI) and Session B (web UI + menubar). Each entry
references its origin cluster/ticket; commit bodies have richer context.

Three priority bands: **MVP-required** (must ship for v2.0 — should be
empty if Phase Z closed cleanly), **Post-MVP / v2.1** (planned next
iteration), **Deferred / nice-to-have** (no current commitment).

Z-7 harvest snapshot: 18 distinct followups across both sessions. MVP-
required band empty as expected (Z-1 + Z-4 closed real bugs as part of
their own tickets; no MVP-blocker carried into Z-7).

---

## MVP-required

_(empty)_

If anything surfaces here at re-harvest, it represents work that should
have resolved before Phase Z cluster close.

| `MB-F-CONSOLE-T01-STDIN-SEQ-PERSIST` | §4.7.1 says "Counters are session-scoped and persist across daemon restarts via the cc_console_buffer table". cc_console_buffer stores stdout rows only; CONSOLE-T01 ships stdin_seq as in-memory only (resets at daemon restart). Add a `cc_console_state` table (or extend cc_console_buffer with a direction column) to persist stdin_seq across restarts. Operator-impact is minor (stdin_seq is for write-ack correlation; clients don't typically rely on its monotonicity across restart) but the contract literal wording does require this. Tier 2. | CONSOLE-T01 green |

| `MB-F-CONSOLE-T01-PIPE-PANE-HIGH-WATER-PAUSE` | §4.7.3 says PTY reader "pauses if no subscribers remain AND ring buffer hits its high-water mark". CONSOLE-T01 ships always-keep-reading even with zero subscribers (simpler upper bound; memory bounded by cap-and-prune loop). Optimization: pause pipe-pane when zero subscribers AND buffer ≥ 0.95 × cap, resume on subscribe. Saves CPU under always-on daemon scenarios. Tier 3. | CONSOLE-T01 green |

---

## Numbering convention

Followups carry a domain prefix matching their package owner:

- `DAEMON-F-*` — `packages/dispatch-daemon/`
- `CLI-F-*` — `packages/dispatch-cli/`
- `WEB-F-*` — `packages/dispatch-web/`
- `UI-F-*` — Session B's UI surface broadly (web + menubar)
- `MB-F-*` — `packages/dispatch-workstation/` (v3.0 Workstation surface)

Origin column references the cluster ID (`D-1` through `D-6`, `W-1`
through `W-5`, `CLI-T`, `Z-*`) plus the originating ticket where
unambiguous. Commit bodies in the originating commits carry full
context; this index is a scan target, not a substitute.

When the daemon exposes `/v3/sessions` or equivalent, fetch active sessions and populate `daemonState` in the `buildContext()` call. The fixed-1s loop is fine in dev; under daemon outages it would generate a steady connection storm. | `MB-F-CONSOLE-T03-MENU-SUBSCRIPTION` | **PARTIALLY CLOSED by Fix-C (cairn finding #82 PARTIAL-RESOLVED at 320f707).** Subscribe wiring shipped: `subscribeConsoleMenuToDaemon` in `console-mount.ts` ships the operator-arbitrated hybrid pattern (bootstrap GET /v2/sessions + WS /v2/events/stream as "something changed" trigger + 150ms debounce). Visual closure NOT achieved — finding #89 (`MB-F-WORKSTATION-MENU-REBUILD-NO-OP`) discovered during Fix-C integration verification: `Menu.setApplicationMenu` post-ready does not propagate to the macOS menu bar regardless of subsequent `rebuildApplicationMenu` calls. Full closure of this followup gated on resolution of #89. Original triage retained for context: The CC Console native menu is rebuilt on demand via `refreshConsoleMenu(sessions)` in main.ts but the daemon-session-list subscription (so the menu auto-refreshes when /v2/sessions changes) is unwired. Initial app start hands `[]` so the menu shows the disabled "No sessions registered" placeholder until something calls `refreshConsoleMenu(['s1', 's2'])`. Wiring options: (a) periodic GET /v2/sessions poll (simple, latency-bounded), (b) /v2/events/stream subscription (push-based, lower-latency). Recommend (b) since the daemon already streams events; this matches the kanban refresh pattern. Tier 2 — land before v3.0 ship-gate so the operator-driven menu open path actually surfaces sessions. | CONSOLE-T03 green → PARTIALLY CLOSED Fix-C `320f707` (gated on cairn #89) |
| `MB-F-CONSOLE-T03-MULTI-PANEL` | **CLOSED by MB-T12 WB4 (2026-05-07).** CONSOLE-T02 ConsoleIpcController tracks up to `panelCap` panels (default 4 per vision §10.11 Q3) and forwards events independently per session. The CONSOLE-T03 React surface was originally single-panel: state held one `sessionName`, opening a second session rebound (overwrote) the first in the visible UI. Closure approach (per Q-MBT12-6=a operator-arbitrated): `ConsolePanel` now takes a required `targetSessionName: string` prop set by its tile-grid parent (one ConsolePanel per tile). Bridge listeners filter on `p.sessionName === targetSessionName` for all 5 channels (open/close/stdout-chunk/gap/error). State shape changed from `{sessionName, gap, error}` to `{open, gap, error}` where `open: boolean` tracks bound/unbound for THIS targetSessionName. The single-panel auto-mount in `console-panel/mount.ts` was deprecated (the tile-grid React tree from MB-T12 WB6 calls `mountConsolePanel` programmatically per tile). 7 new multi-mount tests (probe-01-three-panels-no-crosstalk.spec.tsx) verify: idle states, single-bind, multi-bind without unbind, stdout fan-out filter, gap fan-out filter, listener count = 5×N, close-unbinds-only-target. Vision §10.10 ship-gate ("Two simultaneous CC-console panels bound to different sessions both render correctly with no cross-talk") — VERIFIED. The original "tab strip / Map<sessionName, PerPanelState>" approach was rejected in favor of the simpler "one ConsolePanel React instance per tile" model that aligns with §3.3 spec. Tier 2 (CLOSED). | MB-T12 WB4 (2026-05-07) |

The panel therefore renders nowhere visible in v3.0 ship without this last-mile wiring. UX placement is an operator decision: (a) embed alongside chat panel as a third region in the splitter, (b) replace chat panel via tab strip, (c) separate Electron BrowserWindow opened on demand. Recommend (a) for v3.0 simplicity. Tier 1 — blocks the v3.0 §10.10 ship-gate ("operator opens CC-console panel for a registered session"). | CONSOLE-T03 green |

| `MB-F-MB-T05-PATH-ALLOWLIST-CLAUDE-RESOLUTION` | **CLOSED by batch-6 Session A (cairn finding #72).** Dogfooded 2026-05-03: spawn-from-workstation appeared to succeed (modal closed, no error toast) but tmux session never started; daemon record orphaned. Root cause: `spawn-env.ts` `ALLOWLIST_PATH` excludes `~/.local/bin` (Anthropic official-installer location); tmux exited 0 then claude failed to exec. Permanent fix lands `packages/dispatch-workstation/src/main/binary-resolver.ts` with `resolveClaudeBin()`; `defaultSpawnHandlerDeps` resolves the absolute claude path at workstation startup; tmux argv now passes the absolute path verbatim, bypassing PATH lookup. Unresolved-bin guard in `spawnSession` surfaces SpawnFailed up-front when resolution fails. Tier 1 ship-gate blocker; KNOWN-validated for operator's specific environment, MODELED for other install paths (path-agnostic fix). | MB-T05 green → CLOSED batch-6 Session A |
| `MB-F-MB-T05-POST-SPAWN-LIVENESS-CHECK` | **CLOSED by batch-6 Session A (cairn finding #73).** Defense-in-depth companion to #72. Architectural assumption being repaired: tmux's `new-session -d` exit-0 means session-created, NOT program-running. Without a post-spawn check, any future failure mode where claude dies on exec (corrupt binary, license refused, immediate crash) would leave an orphaned daemon record. Permanent fix: between `runTmuxNewSession` and daemon `registerSession`, sleep `livenessCheckDelayMs` (default 500ms in production, 0ms in unit tests) then run `tmux has-session -t <name>`; on rejection throw SpawnFailed and skip daemon registration. New `runTmuxHasSession` dep + `livenessCheckDelayMs` knob threaded through `SpawnHandlerDeps`. Tier 1 defense-in-depth; 500ms heuristic flagged for measurement-driven tuning in a future ticket. | MB-T05 green → CLOSED batch-6 Session A |
| `MB-F-MB-T05-SPIKE-ENVIRONMENT-VALIDATION` | **CLOSED by batch-6 Session A (cairn finding #74).** Methodology amendment only; no code. MB-S02 spike (basis for `spawn-env.ts` ALLOWLIST_PATH) was run on a Homebrew-only system and baked the assumption "claude lives at /opt/homebrew/bin" into production code without enumerating the install-method variation space. Lesson: spike evidence about operator-installed binaries needs validation across documented install methods, not the spike-runner's environment alone. Binding decision (filed at `docs/adr/MB-S02-spike-environment-validation-amendment.md`): future spikes touching operator-environment-dependent assumptions MUST enumerate the install/configuration variation space and explicitly mark un-validated variations as SPECULATIVE in the ADR. Binds future spikes; does NOT retroactively re-open MB-S02. Tier 2 methodology. | MB-T05 green → CLOSED batch-6 Session A |
| `MB-F-PARALLEL-CAIRN-SCHEMA-FILE-MERGE-CONFLICT` | When two parallel-cairn branches both add schemas to the same file but cut from different parents (one branch cuts from the common ancestor before the other has merged), the second-merging branch hits a content conflict. Today's MB-T10 merge to main hit this conflict; resolved via rebase-then-merge with content-additive resolution. Add to parallel-cairn coordination prompt template: "Second-merging schema-file branch must rebase onto post-first-merge main BEFORE attempting its own merge to main." Discoverability: parallel-cairn coordination doc. Tier 1. | sess-mbt10 merge |
| `MB-F-CC-STREAM-IDLE-TIMEOUT-MID-COMMIT` | Long CC sessions can timeout mid-execution between file-write and commit. Per-commit-push discipline is the load-bearing safety net — because each WB ends with commit+push, a stream timeout interrupting between WB4 close and WB5 commit leaves zero ambiguity (file on disk, branch HEAD = WB4, no half-state). Today's MB-T10 WB5 was recovered cleanly via verify-existing-file path (read the orphan file, validate against probe expectations, integrate, commit). Recovery prompt template should default to verify-existing rather than regenerate when a recovered uncommitted artifact exists. Discoverability: recovery prompt template + chat-layer SOP. Tier 1. | sess-mbt10 WB5 recovery |

Stale rows accumulate over time — minor concern (rows are tiny, indexed on session_name, no GET-path cost), but a tidy session re-spawn under the same name could pick up the stale policy unexpectedly. The post-WB12 shell loads BOTH console-panel/renderer.js (for the WB11 detached-window standalone path) AND tile-grid/renderer.js — both bundles include their own copies of React. | `MB-F-PARALLEL-CAIRN-INDEX-RACE-ATOMIC-COMMIT` | **Tier 1 methodology**. In shared-working-tree parallel-cairn (multiple sessions on the same checkout), the git index is process-shared, NOT session-private. Per-path `git add <path>` (CLAUDE.md §2.7) protects against THIS session's accidental sweeps but does NOT protect against another session running `git add` between this session's pre-commit `git status --short` snapshot and this session's `git commit`. Evidence: MB-T18 WB1 commit `ecdd0e4` was authored as a 4-path MB-T18 scaffold; this session's pre-stage `git --no-pager diff --cached --stat` confirmed exactly 4 paths staged AND pre-commit `git status --short` confirmed the same 4 staged + 7 unstaged from Terminal A's MB-T17 WB4; between that snapshot and the `git commit` invocation, Terminal A staged their 7 WB4 paths from a parallel shell, and the resulting commit captured all 11 paths under MB-T18's subject (see `docs/coordination/mb-t18-wb1-index-race-clarification-2026-05-07.md`). **Forward fix:** every cairn-grammar commit chains `git add → diff --cached --name-only verify → commit` in a SINGLE shell invocation so no other session can mutate the index between verify and commit. Pattern: `git add <paths> && git diff --cached --name-only \| sort > /tmp/intended-staged.txt && printf '<paths>\n' \| sort > /tmp/intended-list.txt && diff /tmp/intended-staged.txt /tmp/intended-list.txt && git commit -m "..." && git --no-pager log -1 --name-only && git push origin main`. The `diff` aborts the chain if the index has more or fewer paths than expected. Q7 self-check protocol revised to verify against `git log -1 --name-only` POST-commit (not pre-commit `git status` snapshot). **Ceiling consideration:** 4 concurrent sessions in a shared working tree create N choose 2 = 6 pairwise race opportunities at every commit; the atomic-chain pattern eliminates the race INSIDE a session but does not address index-write contention between simultaneously-running atomic chains. If 4-session shared-tree parallel-cairn becomes load-bearing, prefer `git worktree` split (CLAUDE.md §4.3 — "each session works in its own worktree") for structural isolation. Cross-reference: `MB-F-T20-LOCAL-HEAD-DIVERGENCE-MECHANISM` (line 130, Tier 3) is Terminal C's downstream observation of this same incident filed under "root cause unidentified"; this followup IDENTIFIES the root cause. Discoverability: this row + clarification doc + ecdd0e4 commit body Q7 + this session's incident-recovery audit thread.  → **Second occurrence at MB-T21 Phase 1 (`e02aa52`, 2026-05-07)**: Terminal A MB-T21 atomic chain ran `git diff --cached --name-only` confirming exactly 1 path staged (`mb-t21-diagnose`); between the diff verify and `git commit` (the `&&` boundary), Terminal C's parallel atomic chain `git add`-ed `mb-t26-diagnose-2026-05-07.md`; my commit + push captured both paths under MB-T21's subject + Q1-Q9 self-check (Q7 "Touched files another parallel session might modify? No" now factually wrong on origin/main — same shape as ecdd0e4). Operator disposition: **accept-and-document** (no force-push, no revert; both files are correct content, only commit attribution misleading). **Forward mitigation per operator (parallel-cairn coordination protocol update 2026-05-07):** at every subsequent atomic-chain, halt ~30 seconds before `git pull --ff-only` to allow other terminals' chains to complete (probabilistic, not guaranteed). Per-path discipline + atomic-chain + courtesy-delay all upheld at MB-T21 WB1-4 (`5fff03a`, `536d413`, `fd6b9c5`, `f2b7ccd`); Q7 verified TRUE on post-commit `git log -1 --name-only` for every WB after Phase 1. **Third occurrence triggers all-sessions halt + structural pivot to `git worktree`** per CLAUDE.md §4.3. The courtesy-delay protocol successfully prevented further incidents in MB-T21 across 4 cairn-grammar commits with 3 concurrent terminals. If shared-tree parallel-cairn continues beyond this run, evaluate worktree-split as the durable forward fix. | MB-T18 WB1 recovery 2026-05-07 |
| `MB-F-T18-FOOTER-DAEMON-AUTHORITATIVE-SPAWN-TIME` | MB-T18 WB2 ships uptime as a renderer-side mount-time snapshot (Q-MBT18-3=a): TileFooter captures `Date.now()` at first useEffect render + ticks every 5s. | `MB-F-T26-Q-DISPOSITION-INFEASIBILITY-ROUND-2` | Methodology evidence: pre-arbitrated Q-dispositions can lock in technically-infeasible designs when the disposing parties (operator + arbitrating session) lack full surface knowledge at arbitration time. **Round 2 evidence at MB-T26 WB3:** operator confirmed Q-MBT26-5=c (polling via existing `coarchitect:` IPC, "preserves preload.mts UNCHANGED invariant") at HALT 0; mid-WB3 authoring revealed that preload.mts has NO generic `invoke(channel, ...)` method — every IPC channel is an explicit named method on `coarchitectBridge`. With Electron `contextIsolation: true`, the renderer cannot reach `ipcRenderer.invoke` directly. So a new `coarchitect:getDailyCost` handler is unreachable WITHOUT adding a new bridge method. Q-MBT26-5=c was infeasible-as-arbitrated. Halt + surface (CLAUDE.md §2.5 + §2.10 honored) led to operator re-disposition Q-MBT26-5=d (push-based via `onCostUpdate` method, +1 line preload.mts addition). Operator recognition: "Q-MBT26-5 re-disposition mid-arbitration was the right call." Methodology gap: §3.10 Q-disposition arbitration is brittle to surface-knowledge gaps — NOT a failure of §3.10, a documented failure mode of the primitive. Closes by adding a §3.10 amendment: arbitrating sessions MUST surface mid-WB if a Q-disposition discovered to be infeasible-as-arbitrated; re-arbitration is operator-arbitrated, not session-pivoted. Tier 2 — methodology hardening. Discoverability: `docs/coordination/mb-t26-findings-2026-05-07.md` §V row + §VIII.4 + WB3 commit `09b38ce` body. | MB-T26 WB4 2026-05-07 |
| `MB-F-PARALLEL-CAIRN-WORKING-TREE-BLOCKING` | **Tier 1 methodology — distinct shared-tree failure mode from index-race.** Index-race (`MB-F-PARALLEL-CAIRN-INDEX-RACE-ATOMIC-COMMIT` Tier 1) protects against sweeps from another session's pre-staged work between this session's diff-verify and commit. **Working-tree-blocking is the inverse:** another session's *uncommitted authored work* in a file this session needs to author against forces this session to either (a) sweep foreign content into own commit (same shape as e02aa52 in reverse), (b) halt until other session commits, or (c) pivot to git-worktree for structural isolation. Atomic-chain does NOT prevent this — diff-verify confirms paths, not content provenance, and the file is legitimately this session's territory to edit. **Validated at MB-T26 mid-WB3:** Terminal B's uncommitted MB-T22 WB2 (chat-shell.tsx + mount.ts) blocked Terminal C's WB3 design; Terminal C halted + surfaced; operator confirmed B's a08b406 had landed; resumed authoring against the committed shape. Workaround in shared-tree contexts: halt-and-coordinate. **Forward fix: git worktree-per-session** per CLAUDE.md §4.3 — only structural defense. Closes by either (i) operator pivots all parallel-cairn sessions to worktree isolation as next-dispatch-wave precondition (recommended; Round 3 trigger threshold met per operator note), OR (ii) protocol amendment requiring all sessions to commit-on-pause (no uncommitted dirty trees crossing session boundaries). Tier 1 — structural methodology. Discoverability: `docs/coordination/mb-t26-findings-2026-05-07.md` §V incident #2 + §VIII.2. | MB-T26 WB4 2026-05-07 |
| `MB-F-PARALLEL-CAIRN-SHARED-TREE-CONTENT-SWEEP` | **Tier 1 methodology — third distinct shared-tree failure mode, NEW at this WB4.** Both sessions have legitimate uncommitted edits to the SAME file. Each session reads, edits its own zone, runs atomic-chain. Diff-verify confirms the path IS expected (each session legitimately edits the file). But `git add <file>` captures the FULL working-tree state of the file — including the OTHER session's uncommitted edits. **Atomic-chain diff-verify protects path-set contention, NOT content provenance** when both sessions have uncommitted edits to the same file. **Incident SHA: 09b38ce** (Terminal C MB-T26 WB3) — preload.mts captured Terminal B's MB-T22 commits-bridge zone (lines 158-179) along with Terminal C's MB-T26 cost-meter bridge zone (lines 32-55). Terminal B's WB3 commit body (`427c6a3`) explicitly cites the `09b38ce content-sweep`. **Preconditions:** two sessions authoring sentinel zones in same file in same window. **Failure surface:** commit attribution captures content from both sessions; cairn audit trail anomalous. Terminal C's WB3 Q7 self-check claim *"Zero T22 territory bleed at MY end"* was [INACCURATE] in retrospect. **Forward fix: git worktree-per-session** per CLAUDE.md §4.3 — only structural defense; per-file edit isolation is workaround for shared-tree contexts (e.g., per-section read-back of file before stage to verify only own zones are dirty). Closes by operator pivoting parallel-cairn sessions to worktree isolation. Same shape as Terminal B's WB3 report named it. Tier 1 — structural methodology. Discoverability: `git --no-pager show 09b38ce -- packages/dispatch-workstation/src/main/preload.mts` shows both MB-T26 zone (32-55) AND MB-T22 commits-bridge zone (158-179) under MY commit + `docs/coordination/mb-t26-findings-2026-05-07.md` §V incident #3 + §VIII.3 + Terminal B's `427c6a3` commit subject `(preload.mts cross-session-swept at 09b38ce — incident cited)`. | MB-T26 WB4 2026-05-07 |
| `MB-F-§3.11-COURTESY-DELAY-HARNESS-INCOMPATIBILITY-ROUND-2` | **Tier 3 methodology — Round 2 evidence of harness-conditional §3.11 executability.** Operator-prescribed §3.11 courtesy delay (~30s halt before atomic-chain to reduce cross-session race probability) is harness-blocked across all forms in **Claude Code 2.1.133** for Terminal C's session: standalone `sleep 30`, chained `sleep 30 && ...`, chained `sleep 30 && echo`, all return *"Blocked: ... use Monitor with an until-loop"*. Terminal A in same Claude Code version executed `sleep` chains successfully — same Claude Code version, divergent harness behavior. **§3.11 is per-session-conditional, not universally executable.** Atomic-chain diff-verify (CLAUDE.md §3.9) is the load-bearing protection; §3.11 is probabilistic mitigation layered on top. Closes by §3.11 amendment in operator-side methodology doc (operator-only territory; Terminal C's WB4 just files this evidence row). Tier 3 — methodology hygiene. Discoverability: Terminal C's WB1+WB2+WB3 commit bodies cite the harness-block + the Round 2 evidence + `docs/coordination/mb-t26-findings-2026-05-07.md` §V row. | MB-T26 WB4 2026-05-07 |
| `MB-F-T27-MODEL-FIELD-PLUMB-FROM-SPAWN` | MB-T27 WB2 ships MixIndicator + MixIndicatorContainer with end-to-end correct subscription wiring (probe-02 9/9 GREEN: bridge subscribe + idempotent dedup + cleanup), but in v3.0 production the indicator renders all chips at 0 even when N>0 sessions live because `TileGridSessionEntry.model` is currently always `undefined`. Root cause: `spawn-handler.ts:387-393` returns only `{sessionName, sessionId, panelMounted, cwd}` from the spawn-result envelope; no model field. The renderer-side guard in `tile-grid-app.tsx:114-134` (`isSpawnSuccessReply`) and the analogous guard in `mix-indicator.tsx` both validate only `{sessionName}` (model field is read defensively for forward-compat but never present). Closure path: (a) extend `SpawnHandler.spawnSession` return type to carry `model?: string`; (b) plumb the active model from the daemon's session record OR from the spawn request (operator decides which side owns it); (c) update `SpawnSuccessReply` guard in tile-grid-app.tsx + mix-indicator.tsx to surface the field. **Operator-arbitrated frozen-schema change required** per CLAUDE.md §2.10 if the daemon spawn-result schema gains a model field. Note: tile-header model chip currently defaults to `'claude-sonnet-4-6'` stub (`tile-header.tsx:137`) — same gap; same closure. Discoverability: `packages/dispatch-workstation/src/chat-shell/mix-indicator.tsx` isSpawnSuccessReply + `packages/dispatch-workstation/src/main/spawn-handler.ts:387-393` + `docs/coordination/mb-t27-findings-2026-05-07.md` §III + Phase 1 diagnose §II-D. | MB-T27 WB3 2026-05-07 |
| `MB-F-T27-KILL-EVENT-PROPAGATION` | MB-T27 WB2 ships MixIndicatorContainer with subscription to `workstationBridge.onSpawnResult` for spawn-side updates, but no equivalent IPC channel exists for kill events. `workstationBridge.killSession` is renderer→main invoke; main has no broadcast back to other renderers when a session is killed via tile-grid UI. Acceptance criterion *"indicator updates within 1s of session kill"* is honestly NOT met in v3.0 — chips do not decrement when sessions are killed. Q-MBT27-4=a operator-arbitrated at HALT 0 (2026-05-07) accepted the gap with this followup. Closure path: (a) author `workstation:session-killed` IPC channel in main process; (b) main fires `webContents.send('workstation:session-killed', { sessionName })` after each successful kill (`session-kill-ipc.ts:135`) AND after detached-tile session ends; (c) preload.mts exposes `workstationBridge.onSessionKilled(cb): () => () => void` mirroring the onSpawnResult pattern; (d) `MixIndicatorBridge` + `MixIndicatorContainer` gain `onSessionKilled` field; (e) container removes session from list on kill event. Same channel could close `MB-F-CONSOLE-T03-MENU-SUBSCRIPTION`'s session-list invalidation gap. Tier 2 — closes acceptance gap; v3.1 closure paired with `MB-F-T27-MODEL-FIELD-PLUMB-FROM-SPAWN`. | `MB-F-PARALLEL-CAIRN-STASH-CROSS-SESSION-RECOVERY` | **Tier 2 methodology — actor-side perspective on cross-session stash interaction; pairs with `MB-F-PARALLEL-CAIRN-CROSS-SESSION-STASH-DESTRUCTIVE` (Tier 1, recipient-side, B's WB5 forthcoming).** MB-T27 WB2 (Terminal D) faced shared-working-tree blocker: B's MB-T22 WB4 WIP (commits-tab integration in `mount.ts` + `commits-tab.tsx` + new test fixture) was uncommitted in the working tree; D's `mount.ts` edits would sweep B's content into D's commit (`MB-F-PARALLEL-CAIRN-SHARED-TREE-CONTENT-SWEEP` Tier 1 shape). Per operator-arbitrated Path β, D stashed B's WIP via explicit-paths `git stash push -u` (named *"B WB4 WIP — MB-T22 commits-tab integration (stashed by Terminal D for MB-T27 WB2 green)"*; preserved-not-deleted). B's atomic-chain mid-flight saw the disappearance as `git commit` exit-1 *"nothing added"* — easily misinterpretable as B's own staging mistake. B held discipline (anti-fabrication §3.1 + halt §3.7), investigated via independent commands, recovered cleanly, shipped at `226c2e9` with full incident citation in commit subject. **Methodology lesson is structural, not behavioral**: even with both sessions executing perfect cairn discipline, the incident still occurred. The failure mode exists because shared working tree allows one session to act on another session's working state at all. Stash isn't safe in shared-working-tree parallel-cairn when (a) sister sessions can recover independently (race condition on stash@{0}), or (b) sister session may misinterpret disappearance as their own staging mistake. Actor-side rule: **don't stash other sessions' work even when blocked** — prefer halt-and-coordinate (`MB-F-PARALLEL-CAIRN-WORKING-TREE-BLOCKING`'s recommended workaround) over reversible-but-still-cross-session-destructive action. Closes by operator pivoting parallel-cairn sessions to git worktree isolation per CLAUDE.md §4.3 — only structural defense. Tier 2 — structural methodology. Discoverability: `git stash list` is empty post-incident (B's recovery dropped the entry); `226c2e9 green(MB-T22): WB4 — ... + Tier-1 stash-recovery incident cited` + `docs/coordination/mb-t27-findings-2026-05-07.md` §IV actor-side perspective + same family as `MB-F-PARALLEL-CAIRN-WORKING-TREE-BLOCKING` Tier 1. | MB-T27 WB3 2026-05-07 |

| `MB-F-PARALLEL-CAIRN-CROSS-SESSION-STASH-DESTRUCTIVE` | **Tier 1 methodology — fourth distinct shared-tree failure mode, NEW at MB-T22 WB5.** **Failure mode:** session B responds to a working-tree-blocking condition (`MB-F-PARALLEL-CAIRN-WORKING-TREE-BLOCKING` row 213) by autonomously running `git stash` on session A's uncommitted WIP to clear B's own blocker — a unilateral cross-session destructive action. Even though `git stash` is reversible via `pop`, it breaks A's atomic-chain mid-flight: between A's `git add` (DIFF_OK printed) and A's `git commit`, B's stash command empties A's index + working tree. A's `git commit` returns exit 1 with `nothing added to commit but untracked files present` — a misleading failure surface easily misdiagnosed as upstream rebase issue or own-staging mistake. **Incident SHA: 226c2e9** (Terminal B MB-T22 WB4) — Terminal D autonomously stashed B's 3 WB4 paths (commits-tab.tsx +192 net, mount.ts +52 net, integration test +402 NEW) with stash message `B WB4 WIP — MB-T22 commits-tab integration (stashed by Terminal D for MB-T27 WB2 green)` to clear the working tree for D's MB-T27 WB2 green-integration. B halted + surfaced per CLAUDE.md §2.9 + §2.5; recovered via `git stash list` → `git stash show -p stash@{0}` → `git stash pop stash@{0}` (no merge conflict; D had not pushed WB2 yet) → re-verified 83/83 scoped tests GREEN → re-ran atomic chain successfully on second attempt. **Distinct from row 213 (working-tree-blocking) which is the BLOCKER condition; row 214 (content-sweep) which is two-sessions-edit-same-file.** This row is the **destructive RESPONSE** to the row 213 blocker. **§3.15 ceiling consideration:** this primitive can be self-imposed by behavioral discipline (don't act on cross-session territory; halt + surface to operator when blocked) but **cannot be enforced without structural isolation**. Expect occurrence in any future shared-tree parallel-cairn run beyond N=2 sessions: more sessions → more likely at least one encounters a working-tree-blocking condition → more likely one of those chooses `git stash` over halt-and-coordinate. **Forward fix: git worktree-per-session per CLAUDE.md §4.3** — only structural defense. Alternative: per-session-id-tagged stash-refusal git hook that aborts `git stash` if the working tree contains another session's WIP (detected via session-id markers in commit-author or env). Closes by operator pivoting parallel-cairn to worktree isolation (recommended) OR by formal protocol amendment forbidding cross-session `git stash/checkout/restore/reset` even when reversible. Cross-references: row 196 `MB-F-PARALLEL-CAIRN-INDEX-RACE-ATOMIC-COMMIT` (mode 1 path-set sweep), row 213 `MB-F-PARALLEL-CAIRN-WORKING-TREE-BLOCKING` (mode 2 BLOCKER), row 214 `MB-F-PARALLEL-CAIRN-SHARED-TREE-CONTENT-SWEEP` (mode 3 content-sweep), this row (mode 4 destructive response). FOUR distinct shared-tree failure modes documented in 24h confirms the §3.17 conclusion: shared-tree contention requires structural isolation, not behavioral discipline alone. Tier 1 — structural methodology. Discoverability: `git stash list` (would have shown the stash entry); `git reflog --all` (shows the stash creation reflog `01d4f9f refs/stash@{0}: On main: B WB4 WIP — MB-T22 commits-tab integration (stashed by Terminal D for MB-T27 WB2 green)`); MB-T22 WB4 commit `226c2e9` body §3.18 INCIDENT section with full T+0 to T+~31s sequence reconstruction; `docs/coordination/mb-t22-findings-2026-05-07.md` §VI postmortem. **Operator-territory amendment flagged:** Round 3 evidence document at `/mnt/user-data/outputs/cairn-under-stress-round-3.md` needs amendment with §3.18 mode-4 before next dispatch wave. | MB-T22 WB5 2026-05-07 |

The orphan validator (WB6) cannot catch dropped refs — they never enter `task.dependsOn` to be checked. Operator-author typos in dep refs surface as silent missing-edges instead of explicit errors. Closes by tightening `parseDependsOn` to surface `task.malformed-field` errors for unparseable comma-list entries (signature change: returns `{ ids: TaskId[]; errors: ParseError[] }` instead of just `TaskId[]`; caller wires errors into the per-task error stream). Discoverability: this row + `packages/dispatch-core/src/build-doc-parser/parser.ts:parseDependsOn` (comment "Unparseable refs silently dropped at WB4...") + `docs/coordination/mb-t28-findings-2026-05-08.md` §VI. | MB-T28 WB-final 2026-05-08 |
| `MB-F-T28-FILE-ENDING-MID-GOAL-WITHOUT-NEWLINE` | **Tier 3 — v3.1 polish.** MB-T28 WB7 dead-code-removal pass eliminated the trailing-flush at end of `parseTaskBody` loop (mid-loop blank-flush handles all well-formed fixtures because trailing newline produces a blank line at section end). Edge case: a BUILD.md file ending exactly on a Goal line (or Goal continuation line) with NO trailing newline AND no following H1/H2/H3 boundary would lose the final line — `goalLines` remains non-null at loop exit but is no longer flushed. All standard editors add trailing newlines; the edge case is constructed (e.g., `printf '...goal text'` with no `\n`). Closes by either (a) restoring the trailing flush with a coverage-justification comment (re-adds 2 dead lines under standard fixtures), OR (b) accepting the edge case as documented degraded behavior (current state). Discoverability: this row + `packages/dispatch-core/src/build-doc-parser/parser.ts` `// No trailing-flush:` comment + `docs/coordination/mb-t28-findings-2026-05-08.md` §VI. | MB-T28 WB-final 2026-05-08 |

Terminal C (MB-T28) ran 9 commits across Phase 1 + WB1-WB7 with ZERO shared-tree incidents: every `git pull --rebase --autostash origin mbt28-worktree` returned "Already up to date" (no other writers to the branch by construction); every atomic-chain diff-verify matched intended-paths exactly; no stash-recovery, no foreign-content sweep, no working-tree-blocking, no index-race. Atomic-chain diff-verify discipline retained value as protection against own-staging mistakes; cross-session contention reduced to push-rebase contention only (which never materialized for Terminal C since each session pushed to its own branch). **Conclusion:** worktree isolation per Round 4 §3.12 pivot eliminates the shared-tree failure modes structurally (as predicted by `MB-F-PARALLEL-CAIRN-CROSS-SESSION-STASH-DESTRUCTIVE` row 220 §3.18 ceiling-consideration paragraph). Each handler must check `approvalRequired` against the session policy resolver (MB-T11-B) before firing. | `MB-F-T11A-SCHEMA-FORWARD-REF-PATTERN` | **Tier 3 — implementation pattern template for future §3 extensions.** MB-T11-A encountered a TypeScript TDZ (Temporal Dead Zone) constraint: `OrchestratorOutputSchema` discriminated union at §3 line ~246 references sub-schemas by name, and TypeScript `const` declarations are NOT hoisted. Placing new schemas at a separate `§14` section (as originally planned) would cause a ReferenceError at module load — `OrchestratorOutputSchema` would reference `SendPromptToSessionOutputSchema` before its `const` was initialized. **Pattern for future §3 extensions:** add new schemas WITHIN §3, immediately before `OrchestratorOutputSchema`, with a clearly delimited comment block (e.g., `// ─── §3 MB-TXX — <description> ─────`). The existing §3 schemas above remain unchanged; the new schemas slot in between them and the union. Also relevant: new schemas that need to reference deeply-nested §10+ types (like `SendPromptEnvelopeSchema`) CANNOT be included in §3 without relocating those types earlier. The `envelope` field was excluded from `SendPromptToSessionOutputSchema` on this basis — it is an IPC-transport detail added by the action handler at execution time, not part of the orchestrator's output contract. Tier 3 — reusable pattern; future-session knowledge propagation. Discoverability: this row + `packages/dispatch-core/src/v3/schema.ts` comment block at §3 MB-T11-A zone + `docs/coordination/mb-t11a-findings-2026-05-08.md` §III. | MB-T11-A WB-final 2026-05-08 |

| `MB-F-T11B-PREDICT-COMMIT-CREATING-IMPL` | **Tier 2 — Round 6 real heuristic implementation for `predictCommitCreating`.** MB-T11-B WB1 ships `predictCommitCreating(_prompt: string): boolean` in `dispatch-core/src/orchestrator/approval-decision.ts` as a stub returning `false` (conservative default: over-approval rather than under-approval per MB-T11-B §1). The stub causes medium-policy send/assign-task actions to auto-fire even when the prompt will produce a `git commit`, reducing medium policy to effectively "spawn/kill/multi-step/contract-touching only." This is acceptable for v3.0 (graceful-degradation R3: missing predicates yield the most-permissive correct answer; operator sees more approval-free fires than intended but no irreversible action slips through unapproved). **Closure path (Round 6):** implement real `predictCommitCreating` as a classifier over orchestrator prompt text. Implementation sketch: (1) regex-match against commit-signal patterns: `git commit`, `git push`, `cairn-grammar commit body`, `commit:`, `green(`, `red(` prefixes — any pattern that suggests the orchestrator intends to produce a commit as part of the action; (2) optionally add LLM-based classification via a lightweight `claude-haiku-4-5` tool-call against the prompt text for ambiguous cases (spike-and-ADR pattern per CLAUDE.md §2.8 before implementing). Returns `true` when confident, `false` otherwise (conservative). Wire the real implementation into the approval-policy-resolver-shim.ts call site (which currently passes no predicates) OR into the v3.1 direct call-site-rewrite per `MB-F-T11-T13-RESOLVER-CALL-SITE-REWRITE`. Tier 2 — v3.0 ships conservative default (stubs-only); real heuristic materially improves medium-policy UX (fewer spurious auto-fires for commit-creating prompts). Discoverability: `dispatch-core/src/orchestrator/approval-decision.ts:predictCommitCreating` stub comment + `docs/coordination/mb-t11b-findings-2026-05-08.md` §VI + this row. | MB-T11-B WB2 2026-05-08 |
| `MB-F-T11B-PREDICT-CONTRACT-TOUCHING-IMPL` | **Tier 2 — Round 6 real heuristic implementation for `predictContractTouching`.** MB-T11-B WB1 ships `predictContractTouching(_prompt: string, _contractPatterns: string[]): boolean` in `dispatch-core/src/orchestrator/approval-decision.ts` as a stub returning `false`. The stub causes medium-policy send/assign-task actions that would modify frozen-contract files to auto-fire without approval under medium policy (since `willTouchContract` defaults to false when the stub is used). Same graceful-degradation R3 posture as `MB-F-T11B-PREDICT-COMMIT-CREATING-IMPL`. **Closure path (Round 6):** implement real `predictContractTouching` as a pattern-matcher. Implementation sketch: (1) operator maintains a `contractPatterns: string[]` list in workstation settings (file globs or regex patterns identifying frozen-contract files, e.g., `['dispatch-core/src/v3/schema.ts', 'CONDUCTOR_API_CONTRACT.md', 'WORKSTATION_CONTRACT.md']`); (2) `predictContractTouching(prompt, contractPatterns)` scans the prompt text for references to any pattern (substring match, then optionally fuzzy-match); (3) returns `true` when any pattern is found in the prompt with sufficient confidence; (4) the `contractPatterns` list is loaded at workstation startup from the settings store (existing persist pattern at `splitter-state.ts`) and passed into the resolver call site. Wire into approval-policy-resolver-shim.ts or the v3.1 direct call-site-rewrite per `MB-F-T11-T13-RESOLVER-CALL-SITE-REWRITE`. The `contractPatterns` list itself is operator-authored; MB-T11-B ships the hook signature (accepting it as a parameter) and stubs the matching logic. Tier 2 — v3.0 ships conservative default; real heuristic closes the contract-touching gate. Discoverability: `dispatch-core/src/orchestrator/approval-decision.ts:predictContractTouching` stub comment + `docs/coordination/mb-t11b-findings-2026-05-08.md` §VI + this row. | MB-T11-B WB2 2026-05-08 |
| `MB-F-HSO-01-TURN-DISPATCH-SYNCHRONOUS` | **Tier 1 — load-bearing for correctness; pre-production HSO requirement.** SPIKE-HSO-01 scenario 4 established that the CC CLI message queue causes undefined ordering and measurement hazards when multiple prompts are submitted without waiting for completion: 22 of 30 turns showed +0 token delta because measurement fired before the queued response completed. Production HSO dispatch MUST send one orchestrator prompt, wait for full completion (status-bar animation absent + token count stable), THEN send the next prompt. The "one action per turn" protocol in the orchestrator system prompt (§2) implies synchronous dispatch by design, but the production dispatch daemon must enforce it structurally — not rely on the orchestrator's own discipline. Async bulk-dispatch (send N prompts in a tight loop) is an anti-pattern that creates ordering races, measurement hazards, and undefined state under error recovery. **Closure path:** MB-T37 (OrchestratorPoolManager) dispatch loop must use synchronous pattern: send → await READY signal (PTY stream event per CONSOLE-T01) → check token count → send next. CONSOLE-T01 PTY broadcaster is the correct READY signal source (not tmux capture-pane animation absence, which was the spike's brittle proxy). Tier 1 — structural correctness for production HSO. Discoverability: `docs/adr/HSO-01-orchestrator-substrate-viability.md` §Findings F2 + §Scenario4 measurement limitations + `docs/spike-evidence/HSO-01/scenario-4-results.md` §Measurement methodology. | SPIKE-HSO-01 scenario-4 2026-05-08 |
| `MB-F-HSO-01-WEEKLY-RATE-LIMIT-DOGFOOD` | **Tier 1 — deferred validation; required before sustained swarm production deployment.** SPIKE-HSO-01 scenario 5 measured the CC CLI extra-usage overage meter (`/cost` command: $3.80/$200, 0% delta over 10 rounds × 3 sessions). This meter measures overages beyond the flat Max subscription rate — it does NOT measure the weekly rate-limit bar visible in the Claude.ai interface (which the operator observed at "81% weekly" at spike time). The two meters are distinct and the weekly bar is not CLI-accessible. Full-scope scenario 5 (30-min × 3+ peers sustained) was reduced to 10 rounds due to unknown operator-side weekly rate-limit capacity at dispatch time. **Closure path:** during dogfood window, run full-scope scenario 5 with Claude.ai interface open: (a) record weekly usage bar at t=0; (b) run 30-min × 3 sessions of orchestration activity; (c) record weekly bar at t=30-min. Measure actual weekly rate-limit delta. At 6-8 sessions sustained, extrapolate weekly consumption vs. billing cycle capacity. File amended scenario-5 results doc. Until closed, the 0% extra-usage result stands but weekly rate-limit headroom at swarm scale is UNCONFIRMED. Tier 1 — required validation gate before committing to 6+ session sustained swarm operation. Discoverability: `docs/adr/HSO-01-orchestrator-substrate-viability.md` §Scenario5 + §Consequences §Deferred + `docs/spike-evidence/HSO-01/scenario-5-results.md` §Measurement limitations. | SPIKE-HSO-01 scenario-5 2026-05-08 |

| `MB-F-HSO-01-SCENARIO1-PIPE-PANE-RERUN` | **Tier 2 — measurement confirmation; currently unconfirmed 3 of 10 scenario 1 results.** SPIKE-HSO-01 scenario 1 used tmux capture-pane to capture orchestrator [ACTION:type]...[/ACTION] output. If re-run shows <90%, file amended scenario-1-results.md and reassess ADR §Decision S1 threshold. | `MB-F-HSO-01-SCENARIO4-SYNCHRONOUS-RERUN` | **Tier 2 — per-turn growth curve calibration; required for precise §3.4 backstop trigger tuning.** SPIKE-HSO-01 scenario 4 used an automated 30-turn loop that submitted all messages in a fast batch via CC CLI message queue. 22 of 30 turns showed +0 token delta because the measurement script fired before the queued response completed (CC CLI queue racing artifact). Only 8 reliable per-turn measurements were captured. The aggregate rate (571 tokens/turn for repeated scenarios; ~3,800/turn for fresh) is reliable, but the per-turn curve shape (linear? sub-linear over time?) cannot be determined from this data. The §3.4 backstop trigger (D3: token-count at 65%) is calibrated on the blended estimate (~2,500 tokens/turn) which is MODELED, not KNOWN. **Closure path:** re-run scenario 4 synchronously: (a) 10 novel (never-seen) action scenarios, each submitted with explicit wait-for-completion (CONSOLE-T01 PTY stream READY signal per MB-F-HSO-01-TURN-DISPATCH-SYNCHRONOUS); (b) record per-turn token count before and after each response; (c) derive per-turn rate distribution (p25/median/p75). File amended scenario-4-results.md with per-turn curve. Update §3.4 backstop trigger calibration parameters from MODELED to KNOWN. Tier 2 — current MODELED estimate is conservative (using blended rate); production HSO is unlikely to hit a dangerous misfire, but precise calibration avoids overly early handoffs. | `MB-F-MBT37-MID-ACTION-HEURISTIC-AMBIGUITY` | **Tier 3 — Q-MBT37-2c mid-action tag detection; false-positive risk in production prose.** `OrchestratorPoolManager._onPtyChunk` detects mid-action state by checking `chunk.includes('[ACTION:')` (set `_midAction = true`) and `chunk.includes('[/ACTION]')` (clear `_midAction = false`). This heuristic assumes `[ACTION:` and `[/ACTION]` substrings appear exclusively in formal action-block markers emitted by the CC CLI orchestrator. **False-positive risk:** if the active orchestrator writes prose explaining action syntax to a peer (e.g., "you should emit an `[ACTION:send-prompt-to-session]` block"), or if error-recovery output contains action-marker-like substrings, `_midAction` could become stuck at `true`. A stuck `_midAction = true` indefinitely suppresses `[HANDOFF-NOW]` directives — the threshold is crossed but the directive is never sent. Eventual effect: the active orchestrator exhausts its context window with no handoff. **Known mitigation:** the `[HANDOFF-EMITTED]` marker resets `_midAction = false` via `_promote()` — but if the orchestrator never reaches `[HANDOFF-EMITTED]` (because it never sends `[HANDOFF-NOW]` because `_midAction` is stuck), this path is unreachable. **Closure path options:** (a) more precise regex matching `\[ACTION:[a-z-]+\]` and `\[\/ACTION\]` to reduce substring false positives; (b) a hard timeout that clears `_midAction` after N seconds (e.g., 60s); (c) operator override: a new `clearMidAction()` method on `OrchestratorPoolManager` exposed to the UI for manual recovery; (d) structural: track ACTION nesting depth as an integer counter rather than boolean. Tier 3 — production risk exists but requires a specific failure mode (stuck mid-action) to materialize; the token threshold at 130K still allows room for manual intervention. Discoverability: this row + `docs/coordination/mb-t37-findings-2026-05-08.md` §VIII Q-MBT37-2c note + `packages/dispatch-workstation/src/coarchitect/hso-pool.ts:_onPtyChunk`. | MB-T37 WB4 2026-05-08 |
| `MB-F-MBT39-MULTI-CHUNK-RESPONSE-ACCUMULATION` | **Tier 2 — single-chunk response model may not reflect production LLM PTY behavior; load-bearing for production correctness.** `PeerSummaryHarvester._parseResponse` treats each chunk that arrives while `state === 'AWAITING_RESPONSE'` as a complete response candidate — `jsYaml.load(chunk.trim())` is called on the raw chunk text. In production, CC CLI PTY output is streamed token-by-token: a §7 YAML summary will arrive as many small chunks (individual tokens or lines), not as a single complete-response chunk. The test harness delivers `capturedObserver('alpha', fullYamlString)` as one call, which passes. But in production the same YAML will arrive as N chunks over ~1–2 seconds — each intermediate chunk will attempt a `jsYaml.load()` call, get a parse error or partial object, and emit an `error:recorded` before the complete YAML has landed. The TIMEOUT path partially mitigates this: the error emitted on parse failure returns state to IDLE, and the TIMEOUT timer is cancelled — but the peer's turn-complete event is lost and an error is recorded spuriously. The correct architecture for production is: accumulate chunks in a per-peer buffer while `state === 'AWAITING_RESPONSE'`; fire inner-quiescence (a second, shorter debounce) when the buffer has been stable for a configurable window (e.g., 500ms); parse the accumulated buffer at inner quiescence. **Closure path:** add `responseBuffer: string` field to `PeerEntry`; on chunk arrival in `AWAITING_RESPONSE`, append to buffer and reset an inner-quiescence timer; on inner-quiescence, parse the full buffer. Inner quiescence threshold should be shorter than outer (e.g., 500ms vs 3000ms) and configurable via `responseQuiescenceMs` constructor dep. Tier 2 — single-chunk model is sufficient for test harness correctness but will produce systematic spurious errors in any production deployment where PTY output is streamed. This is the highest-priority MB-T39 finding. Discoverability: this row + `docs/coordination/mb-t39-findings-2026-05-08.md` §VIII item 4 + `packages/dispatch-workstation/src/coarchitect/peer-summary-harvester.ts:_parseResponse`. → **CLOSED at `bb4c47c`** — per-peer response buffer + inner-quiescence shipped; 13/13 probes GREEN; `docs/coordination/mb-f-mbt39-multi-chunk-fix-findings-2026-05-09.md` | MB-T39 WB4 2026-05-08 |
| `MB-F-MBT39-BASE64-CHUNK-PASSTHROUGH` | **Tier 3 — observer receives raw PTY bytes without encoding field; base64-encoded chunks are not decoded before YAML parse.** `IConsoleBroadcaster.addStdoutObserver` passes `chunk: string` directly to `_onPtyChunk`. The fan-out in `console-ipc.ts:356` calls `obs(sessionName, bytes)` where `bytes` is the raw PTY output. CC CLI text output is UTF-8 in practice, so this is a no-op concern under normal operation. However, if the PTY emits binary content or if a future version of `addStdoutObserver` adds base64 encoding for transport safety (e.g., to handle null bytes in the IPC channel), the harvester's `jsYaml.load(chunk.trim())` will receive encoded data and fail. **Closure path:** if `addStdoutObserver`'s fan-out contract is amended to include an encoding field (e.g., `obs(sessionName, bytes, encoding?: 'utf8' | 'base64')`), `_onPtyChunk` must decode before parsing. Alternatively, detect `base64:` prefix convention if adopted. No action required until `addStdoutObserver` fan-out contract changes. Tier 3 — edge case; CC CLI text is UTF-8; closure depends on upstream contract amendment by Terminal X territory. Discoverability: this row + `docs/coordination/mb-t39-findings-2026-05-08.md` §IX + `packages/dispatch-workstation/src/coarchitect/peer-summary-harvester.ts:_onPtyChunk`. | MB-T39 WB4 2026-05-08 |
| `MB-F-MBT39-PEER-SESSION-NAME-CROSS-CHECK` | **Tier 3 — by design; YAML `peer_session` not cross-checked against observer `sessionName`; YAML field is informational only.** `_validateSummary` requires `peer_session` to be present and a string (required field validation), but does NOT verify `peer_session === sessionName`. The `PeerTurnCompletePayload` uses the observer's `sessionName` as authoritative (the PTY fan-out knows which session emitted the chunk; the YAML field is self-reported by the peer and could drift if the peer's system prompt uses a stale session name). The by-design decision is recorded in `peer-summary-harvester.ts:307` comment. **Closure path:** log a warning (not error) when `parsed.peer_session !== sessionName` — this surfaces session name mismatches without rejecting the summary. Warning should include both values for operator inspection. Appropriate if operator wants observability into session-name drift without blocking valid summaries. Tier 3 — by-design; no correctness impact on the swarm state; `sessionName` in the emitted payload is always the PTY-authoritative value. Discoverability: this row + `docs/coordination/mb-t39-findings-2026-05-08.md` §VIII item 3 + `packages/dispatch-workstation/src/coarchitect/peer-summary-harvester.ts:_validateSummary`. | MB-T39 WB4 2026-05-08 |
| `MB-F-MBT40-ACTION-MARKER-CARD-RENDERING` | **Tier 3 — Q-MBT40-4(c) deferral; richer card UI for action markers pending Q-MBT21-7=a re-arbitration.** MB-T40 ships `ptyTurnDone` with a plain-text `[ACTION:type]...[/ACTION]` reconstruction (Q-MBT40-4(c)) because importing `OrchestratorCard` from `dispatch-web` would violate Q-MBT21-7=a (no cross-package imports from dispatch-web into dispatch-workstation). Discoverability: this row + `docs/coordination/mb-t40-findings-2026-05-09.md` §VI item 2 + `packages/dispatch-workstation/src/main/pty-stream-relay.ts` Q-MBT40-4(c) comment. | MB-T40 WB4 2026-05-09 |
| `MB-F-MBT40-PTY-MESSAGE-PERSISTENCE` | **Tier 2 — A2 deferral; PTY messages not persisted to daemon DB; cross-restart history lost.** MB-T40 A2 ratification: `onStreamDone(text)` pushes `{ role: 'assistant', content: text }` directly to React history state (`chat-panel.tsx:85–102`) rather than calling `daemonClient.fetchHistory()` after each turn. This is correct per the v3.5 scope — PTY messages are in-session-only. Mount-time `fetchHistory()` is unchanged so pre-session daemon DB history loads correctly. **Consequence:** if the operator closes and reopens the workstation (or the renderer is reloaded), orchestrator PTY turns from the prior session are not recoverable via the history panel. The chat log shows only messages that arrived via the Anthropic API path (which ARE persisted to the daemon DB via `daemonClient.postMessage`) and the current session's PTY turns. **Closure path:** persist orchestrator PTY turns to the daemon DB. Two options: (a) main-process side — have `pty-stream-relay.ts` call `daemonClient.postMessage({ role: 'assistant', content: text })` on each `ptyTurnDone` event; (b) renderer side — have `chat-panel.tsx onStreamDone` call `daemonClient.postMessage` before `setHistory`. Option (a) is cleaner (single persistence point; main-process has daemon client). Requires `daemonClient` accessible in `pty-stream-relay.ts` context; pass via `PtyRelayDeps`. Tier 2 — loss of cross-restart history is a UX regression relative to the AnthropicChatClient path; operator-arbitrated priority for v3.5 vs. v4.0. Discoverability: this row + `docs/coordination/mb-t40-findings-2026-05-09.md` §III A2 row + `packages/dispatch-workstation/src/coarchitect/chat-panel.tsx:85–102`. | MB-T40 WB4 2026-05-09 |
| `MB-F-MBT40-PTY-STREAM-ERROR-WIRING` | **Tier 3 — A3 deferral; PTY session close events not mapped to onStreamError; chat-panel error display not triggered on PTY close.** MB-T40 A3 ratification: `onStreamError` is a no-op stub in `PtyStreamingBridgeImpl` and `preload.mts` (PTY model has no stream error semantics distinct from PTY session lifecycle). The chat-panel's error banner (`data-testid="chat-panel-error"`) is only triggered by `streamingBridge.onStreamError`. If the PTY session for `__orchestrator_active` closes unexpectedly (e.g., Claude Code CLI crash, network interruption, tmux kill-session), the chat panel shows no error — it silently stops receiving chunks. The operator has no in-UI feedback that the PTY stream closed. **Closure path:** wire `ConsoleIpcController` error/close events to a main-process broadcast channel (e.g., `coarchitect:ptyStreamClose`) and subscribe in `preload.mts onStreamError` (replacing the no-op). `PtyStreamingBridgeImpl` would similarly call registered `onStreamError` callbacks when the broadcaster's observer is disposed (observe: `disposeObserver` currently fires on `dispose()` — also fires on broadcaster teardown?). Implementation requires Terminal X (`console-ipc.ts`) to expose a close-event observer tap analogous to `addStdoutObserver`. Tier 3 — PTY close without error display is tolerable for v3.5; operator can observe the PTY tile going silent in the console panel. | `MB-F-HSO-POOL-STANDBY-RESPAWN-UNBOUNDED` | **Tier 3 — pool standby-respawn loops unboundedly when initial spawn permanently fails.** Pre-existing pool design surfaced by WB16 runtime-launch smoke (`b607ed1`) under `SessionCapExceeded`-blocked spawn: console shows 9 standby halt iterations during a ~12s observation window — far more than the nominal `POLL_INTERVAL_MS=5000` would yield. Mechanism per direct `hso-pool.ts` read: `_pollOnce` (line 188-207) detects active-tmux-missing → `_onActiveCrash` (line 264-268) → `_promote()` (line 247-257) which resets `_activeCrashHandled = false` at line 255 → `_spawnAndRegister(RESERVED_STANDBY)` (async, fails with halt). Next poll iteration repeats. The reset-on-promote pattern creates an unbounded cycle when neither active nor standby ever successfully spawned. NOT a WB14 regression — pre-existing pool design from MB-T37 ship. **Closure path:** (a) bound retries via a `_consecutiveSpawnFailures` counter incremented in `_spawnAndRegister` error path and zeroed on success; halt-and-quiesce after N (e.g., 3) failures; (b) operator-visible "pool quiesced; retry via UI" affordance; (c) ensure `_promote` does NOT reset `_activeCrashHandled` when the standby was never successfully spawned. Tier 3 — observable as repeated console noise; no production data corruption; no merge-gate impact (WB16 smoke still PASSED on WINDOW_READY + no-crash criteria). Discoverability: this row + `docs/coordination/wb16-runtime-smoke-2026-05-11.md` §IV-finding-3 + V + `hso-pool.ts:188-207` `_pollOnce` + `hso-pool.ts:247-257` `_promote`. | WB16 smoke evidence 2026-05-11 |

This satisfies all stated acceptance criteria (no retry, no overwrite, operator-visible actionable message). | `MB-F-WORKTREE-SHARED-HEAD-PUSH-SWEEP-2026-05-11` | **Tier 3 — `git push` from any session sweeps EVERY local commit currently on HEAD; pathspec-restricted commit isolates only the INDEX layer, NOT the refs layer.** Refs-layer analog of `MB-F-WORKTREE-SAMEPATH-CROSSSESSION-SWEEP-2026-05-10`. Observed at Phase 4.1 forward-fix: T4's `git push origin main` for a single `docs/FOLLOWUPS.md` commit at `6a40859` pushed THREE commits (T4's `6a40859` + T3's `ab7e093` + T2's `1697e46`) because all three sessions committed locally before T4's push, and `.git/refs/heads/main` is shared single-pointer state. The serial-push ack chain (T4 → T2 → T3) intended by orchestrator was structurally impossible once T2/T3 had committed. Detection: T4's push output showed `27ee149..1697e46` instead of `27ee149..6a40859`; the SHA mismatch surfaced the sweep immediately. **Closure path options:** (a) **worktree isolation** — each parallel session gets its own `git worktree` (`~/Desktop/Automata/foxworks-worktrees/<session-name>/`) per CLAUDE.md §4.3; separate `.git/refs/heads/<branch>` files; no cross-session sweep possible. RECOMMENDED for HSO-automated parallel work. (b) **orchestrator-mediated push** — orchestrator session reads each T-session's HEAD before authorizing push; if HEAD includes commits from other sessions, surface for explicit batch-attribution-ack OR rebase/cherry-pick to isolate. Higher coordination overhead. (c) **accept-the-sweep** — first-pusher-after-everyone-commits sweeps everything; commit bodies must answer Q7 honestly against actual `git log origin/main..HEAD` immediately BEFORE push (re-verifying delta hasn't grown since HALT-PRE-PUSH ack). Cheapest; relies on per-session discipline. Tier 3 — caught at next-session orchestrator inspection; archaeological record preserved in WB14a commit body Q7 + this row; serialization needed for HSO automated parallel work. Discoverability: this row + `MB-T-HSO-WIRE` findings doc §V architecture-notes-pathspec-discipline + `MB-F-WORKTREE-SAMEPATH-CROSSSESSION-SWEEP-2026-05-10` (sibling row, index-layer analog). | Phase 4.1 forward-fix evidence 2026-05-11 |

| `MB-F-DOGFOOD-POOL-STANDBY-RESPAWN-LOOP-UNDER-SESSIONCAP` | **Tier 2 — HSO pool `_onStandbyCrash` loops every `POLL_INTERVAL_MS=5000ms` under `SessionCapExceeded` saturation; 6 standby halt-and-surface emissions to chat banner in a 30s window.** Phase B dogfood at `eb69cc0` observed under operator env with 5 existing tmux sessions (cap saturated): pool's initial `_spawnAndRegister(RESERVED_ACTIVE)` and `_spawnAndRegister(RESERVED_STANDBY)` both failed with `error_type: SessionCapExceeded`. WB13's halt-and-surface path fired correctly for the initial pair (1 active + 1 standby halt). Then the `_startPoll` tmux-poll handler (`POLL_INTERVAL_MS=5000`) detected the missing standby every 5s and re-triggered `_onStandbyCrash` → `_spawnAndRegister(RESERVED_STANDBY)` → failed again with the same `SessionCapExceeded` error → another halt-and-surface emission. Total: 7 halts in 30s (1 active + 6 standby retries). Each halt also emits `coarchitect:streamError` to the renderer's chat error banner — operator-visible spam under saturated session cap. **Closure path:** in `OrchestratorPoolManager._onStandbyCrash`, distinguish terminal failures (`SessionCapExceeded` → back off / disable auto-spawn until operator action) from transient failures (network / daemon flake → exponential backoff). Options: (a) check `error_type` before re-spawning; on `SessionCapExceeded`, set a back-off flag and emit a single halt; (b) stop the poll timer entirely when first spawn fails with `SessionCapExceeded` and require operator action to restart; (c) pool-aware tracking of consecutive identical errors to suppress retry-spam. Related: `MB-F-HSO-POOL-STANDBY-RESPAWN-UNBOUNDED` (Tier 3, filed at WB17 `a600493`) — that row is the architectural concern (unbounded retry); this row is the specific UX manifestation observed in dogfood under realistic SessionCap saturation. Tier 2 — not a correctness bug (halts ARE halt-and-surface; no silent failure) but degrades operator UX under normal-but-saturated environments. Discoverability: this row + `docs/coordination/dogfood-phase-b-2026-05-11.md` §III pool behavior + §V + `MB-F-HSO-POOL-STANDBY-RESPAWN-UNBOUNDED`. | dogfood Phase B 2026-05-11 |
| `MB-F-POOL-SHUTDOWN-HOOK-DAEMON-RECONCILIATION` | **Tier 3 — pool's `stop()` exists at `hso-pool.ts:132-139` but is never called from `main.ts`; clean SIGTERM leaks 2 daemon-registry orphans per workstation launch.** Phase C dogfood at `673d5d6` observed: under clean SIGTERM, OS kills the tmux session tree (`__orchestrator_active` + `__orchestrator_standby`) but `pool.stop()` is never invoked from `main.ts`, so the daemon registry retains `state=armed` for both entries after workstation exit. Each subsequent launch adds 2 more orphans. Phase C pre-launch cleanup observed 9 days of accumulated test/probe fixtures (5 probe-* fixtures + 1 ambiguous `try it`) that share this manifestation pattern across operator-driven + pool-driven flows. Sibling of `MB-F-AUDIT-EXTERNAL-SESSION-DEATH-RECONCILIATION` (row 271 — operator-driven manifestation); this row is the pool-driven manifestation. **Closure path:** (a) register `pool.stop()` in `main.ts` via electron `app.on('before-quit', ...)` handler; (b) extend `pool.stop()` to iterate active+standby session names + `PATCH /v2/sessions/<name>/state {"state":"killed"}` via daemon client BEFORE tmux teardown; (c) optionally, daemon-side reaper for orphaned-armed sessions older than N days (defense-in-depth; broader fix covering operator-driven manifestation too — closes row 271). | `MB-F-SHUTDOWN-HOOK-SIGNAL-EXIT-BYPASS-BEFORE-QUIT` | **Tier 3 — electron signal-handler force-exits before async-await race resolves; SIGTERM/SIGINT bypass `preventDefault()` + async race in `before-quit` handler.** WB3 smoke cycle 1 (`pkill -TERM`) achieved partial PATCH-to-'held' (active='held' but standby='armed'); WB3 smoke cycle 2 (`pkill -INT`) achieved no PATCH (both 'armed'). WB3 cycle 3 (AppleScript-driven Cmd-Q via `osascript`) achieved FULL PASS (both 'held'). Proof the handler IS wired correctly per probe `bd1af53` 5/5 GREEN — the issue is electron's signal-flow bypassing the `event.preventDefault()` + `Promise.race([pool.stop(), timeoutPromise(5_000)])` + `app.exit(0)` pattern. Likely mechanism [MODELED]: OS-level signal handler force-exits the electron process before the main thread's async PATCH calls finish. NOT a WB2 regression — production-target Cmd-Q path works. **Closure path options:** (a) explicit `process.on('SIGTERM', ...)` + `process.on('SIGINT', ...)` handlers that synchronously call `pool.stop()` before letting the signal propagate; (b) operator UX guidance — production teardown uses Cmd-Q (works) not SIGTERM (unreliable); (c) accept-the-edge-case — operator-driven quit is the primary production path; signal-based exit is for dev/CI tooling where state-machine cleanliness is less critical (path-E start-side catches stale 'armed' rows on next launch). Tier 3 — production behavior (Cmd-Q) honest; dev-tool behavior partially-broken-but-recoverable. Discoverability: this row + `docs/coordination/mbt-pool-shutdown-hook-smoke-2026-05-11.md` §IV-finding-3 + §V row body + WB2 commit body (`eaa00c2`) + WB1 probe (`bd1af53`) + `docs/coordination/mbt-pool-shutdown-hook-findings-2026-05-11.md` §IX. | MB-T-POOL-SHUTDOWN-HOOK-FIX WB3 smoke 2026-05-11 |

| `MB-F-PARALLEL-CAIRN-SHARED-INDEX-RACE-WINDOW` | **Tier 1 (per dispatch §5.4 Round 9 stress regime — new findings Tier 1 by default) — shared-working-tree parallel-cairn execution creates an index-race window between `git add` and `git commit`. First commit attempt fails with `error: pathspec ... did not match any file(s) known to git`.** [KNOWN per T2 WB2 commit body `d627096` direct experience 2026-05-12]: T6 sub-session landed two commits (`54d5b58` WB4 + `0d71590` WB5) between T2's per-path `git add` of 3 files and the `git commit` attempt. Sub-session coordination via cross-session coord docs. (β) atomic stage-commit-push sequence helper — single command that runs `git add` + `git commit` + `git push` with index-lock awareness. (γ) accept index-race as inherent cost of shared-working-tree mode; document recovery pattern (re-stage + re-verify before commit). (δ) T6 methodology infrastructure may close this when build-freshness/bundle-fingerprint workflows graduate to atomic-commit support. **Tier 1** per dispatch §5.4 stress regime Round 9 default — methodology gap that affects ALL parallel-cairn sub-sessions. **Discoverability**: this row + T2 WB2 commit body `d627096` parallel-cairn incident section + T2 findings doc §IX + dispatch `4f0bbde` §5.4 (stress regime Tier 1 default) + CLAUDE.md §2.7 (per-path discipline) + CLAUDE.md §4.3 (separate worktree mention). | T2 sub-session WB2 commit body `d627096` 2026-05-12 + WB14 docs filing |
